Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mult_add_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
- output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where `reset` is low, so, for a block whose inputs, `chainin` included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `scanin` = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: `ay` is driven directly, and `scanout` is left unconnected); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. `chainout` reaches only the next block's `chainin`, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.

9 call(s) in 3 passes, in order

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified tpu_like.small.os.fixed, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 288 on 94 DSP tiles, all in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode (50 tiles with 4, 16 with 3, 12 with 2 and 16 with 1). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when you propose no rewrite>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

No rewrite
  • REGIONS: NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the REGIONS line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (16 independent lanes; each block sums only its own product with its own intercept term through bx; no chaining)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one mult_add_int_18x19 per lane gi = 0..15 of module activation: clk = clk; reset = (reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)); mode_sigs = 12'b0; ax = {10'b0, data_slope_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; ay = {11'b0, inp_data_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; chainin = 64'b0; scanin = 19'b0; result[7:0] drives intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: activation_type does not change while the activation unit is processing data, i.e. while (enable_activation=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || activation_in_progress)). Support: lines 5480-5489 — when activation_type==0 the tanh pipeline registers (slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed, intercept_applied_data_internal) are not assigned in that branch and therefore hold; line 5534 — intercept_applied_data_internal is read only when activation_type==1; line 5461 — out_data exposes it only when enable_activation==1. The rewrite reproduces the "clear" and "load" branches exactly; only the frozen-pipeline (ReLU-mode) branch is not reproduced, and it is observable only if activation_type goes 0→1 without the unit leaving the active state in between. Lines 4838/4913 (activation_type resets to 1 and is only changed by an APB write to REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR) and 5259-5262 (the chain only starts on start_tpu) show the CSR is a configuration register written before a run. Note also that a run held entirely in ReLU mode is still exact: intercept_applied_data_internal is unread then (5534), and the first cycle in which the unit leaves the active state drives the block's reset high, so the block's result is 0 again exactly when the original register is 0.

Converted region — the 16 multiplies at line 5482 (one per lane, 1 instance of activation)

What the original does, per lane i, in the one always block at 5465:

  • branch A (reset || ~enable_activation) and branch C (idle): slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, data_intercept_flopped, intercept_applied_data_internal <= 0;
  • branch B with activation_type==1: S <= data_slope_flopped*inp_data_flopped (8-bit, mod 256), dif <= data_intercept, D <= dif, I <= S + D (mod 256).
    S (slope_applied_data_internal) and D (data_intercept_delayed) are read nowhere except the sum at 5485 (verified by searching the whole file), so the pair can be replaced by the single value S+D, and that value, taken two edges after its operands, is exactly what the block's result holds: result = data_slope_flopped*inp_data_flopped + data_intercept_flopped, all three read at the same instant, product and sum truncated to 8 bits by the slice assignment — the same value mod 256 the original computes. With the block's reset driven by the "clear" condition, all three cases line up: clear at edge e → result 0 after e (original I = 0); load at e after a clear at e-1 → result = 0*0+0 = 0 after e (original S=D=0, so I = 0); load at e and e-1 → result = ds_f*idf+dif of the cycle before e (original I = S+D with exactly those values). Operand widths: both operands are unsigned 8-bit, so the 18/19-bit ports are zero-extended and the 37-bit product never wraps; bx carries the intercept byte as the raw 8-bit pattern, as the original 8-bit add does.

Register stages, lane i:

  • Before: data_slope_flopped / inp_data_flopped / data_intercept_flopped → (stage 1) slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed → (stage 2) intercept_applied_data_internal. Two stages.
  • After: the same three signals → (stage 1) the block's ax/ay/bx input registers → (stage 2) the block's result register. Two stages, same latency and same values. The stages feeding the block (inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped at 5450-5458, and data_intercept_flopped at 5483) are unchanged, so inp_data → out_data is still three register stages and out_data_available / done_activation / cycle_count / the ReLU path are untouched.

Blocks added: 16 (one DSP tile each; the design uses 94 DSP tiles today, so the result stays well under the 132 available). Soft logic removed: the 16 8×8 multiplies, the 16 8-bit adds, and 384 flip-flops (slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, intercept_applied_data_internal, 128 bits each).

Edits for the editor (module activation, lines 5417-5626 — change nothing else in the file, and do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19; instantiate it by name):

  1. Line 5433: delete the declaration of slope_applied_data_internal. Line 5444: delete the declaration of data_intercept_delayed. Line 5434: change intercept_applied_data_internal from reg to wire [\DESIGN_SIZE*\DWIDTH-1:0].
  2. In the always block at 5465, delete only these assignments: 5467, 5468, 5470 (branch A), 5482, 5484, 5485 (branch B's tanh arm), 5522, 5523, 5525 (branch C). Keep everything else exactly as it is, in particular data_intercept_flopped <= 0 (5471, 5526) and data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept[i*8 +: 8] (5483), the ReLU arm (5487), cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_activation_internal, activation_in_progress and in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available (5476). The tanh arm of the for loop then contains only line 5483; keep the if(activation_type==1'b1) ... else ... structure as is.
  3. After the always block (before line 5534), add:
wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress);
genvar gi;
generate
for (gi = 0; gi < `DESIGN_SIZE; gi = gi + 1) begin : act_tanh_dsp
  wire [63:0] blk_result;
  wire [63:0] blk_chainout;
  wire [18:0] blk_scanout;
  mult_add_int_18x19 u_act_mul_add (
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(act_blk_reset),
    .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, data_slope_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}),
    .ay({11'b0, inp_data_flopped[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]}),
    .bx({28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}),
    .chainin(64'b0),
    .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(blk_result),
    .chainout(blk_chainout),
    .scanout(blk_scanout));
  assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = blk_result[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

(16 hand-unrolled instances are acceptable if the generate form gives the flow trouble; the connections must be identical.) chainout/scanout are left dangling — the blocks are not chained, so chainin is tied to 0 as required.

Power-up: at time 0 and through the testbench's reset phase, reset=1 and enable_activation=0, so act_blk_reset is high and every block register is cleared before the first compared cycle; no X can reach out_data.

Candidates left this pass

  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368 … $413 (16 multiplies, module norm): left, correctness/timing. The two design stages (mean_applied_data, then variance_applied_data) match the block's two stages, but the block registers both operands at its entry while the design holds only mean_applied_data in a register at that point: inv_var (5010, from the cfg register at 4889) is read live by the multiply. Mapping it to ay would multiply by the previous cycle's inv_var, which is the original's behaviour only under an assumption that inv_var is stable across a normalization run. That assumption is not needed for the activation rewrite, so norm is not chosen this pass; it is the obvious next candidate if the activation result is favourable.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349 (256 instances, the systolic array's PEs): left, two reasons. (a) Capacity: the block takes a DSP tile alone and qmult has 256 instances in one module, so converting the region needs 256 tiles on a device with 132. (b) Even for a subset, the accumulator in seq_mac (4699-4704: out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg, every cycle) cannot use the block's bx as the accumulator feedback: bx is registered at the block's entry, so the loop would add result from two cycles earlier and split the accumulation into two interleaved sums. Using the block only as the multiplier (a_flopped/b_flopped and mul_out_temp_reg are a 2-stage match) would be behaviourally correct, but it needs one tile per PE and so is out on capacity.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two source changes would open the rest of this design to the block. (1) In norm, register inv_var one stage earlier (a local inv_var_flopped sampled with the same clear/enable as mean_applied_data, used in the product at 5070): the multiply would then take both operands from registers at the same stage and map onto the block with no assumption. (2) In activation, assign the tanh pipeline registers (5482-5485) unconditionally on activation_type rather than only in the tanh arm — the ReLU arm leaves them frozen — which would remove the need for the ASSUMES above. (3) The 256 PE MACs would be the real prize, but they need one DSP tile each; a mode that fits several 8×8 MACs per tile, or a device with more DSP columns, would be required.

1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

1	        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
2	        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
3	        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
4	        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
5	        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
6	        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
7	        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
8	        fill_matrices;
9	        phase = "run1";
10	        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
21	        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
22	`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
23	        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
24	`endif
25	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
26	        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
27	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
28	        phase = "run3";
29	        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
30	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
31	        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
32	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
33	        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
34	        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
35	        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
36	            tick; set_data;
37	            case (ph)
38	                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
39	                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
40	                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
41	                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
42	            endcase
43	            ph = (ph + 1) % 4;
44	            draw; bram_we_a_ext = (r32[1:0] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0; bram_we_b_ext = (r32[3:2] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0;
45	            draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % 1024) == 0); resetn = ~reset;
46	        end

1	    // tpu_like.small.ws top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the design driven as its
2	    // software drives it. Matrices written into the two BRAMs through the external ports (word k of A at
3	    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this
4	    // design's address strides reset to 1), the register block configured over APB, start_tpu written,
5	    // done_tpu polled, the 18 words around address_mat_c read back from BRAM A. A multiply writes its
6	    // results once per reset (the accumulator's start_pooling is cleared by resetn alone, the activation
7	    // unit's done count by reset alone), so every run after the first follows a 4-cycle reset of both.

Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

Multiplies in the unmodified tpu_like.small.os.fixed, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 288 on 94 DSP tiles, all in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode (50 tiles with 4, 16 with 3, 12 with 2 and 16 with 1). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (16 independent lanes; each block sums only its own product with its own intercept term through bx; no chaining)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one mult_add_int_18x19 per lane gi = 0..15 of module activation: clk = clk; reset = (reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)); mode_sigs = 12'b0; ax = {10'b0, data_slope_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; ay = {11'b0, inp_data_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; chainin = 64'b0; scanin = 19'b0; result[7:0] drives intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: activation_type does not change while the activation unit is processing data, i.e. while (enable_activation=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || activation_in_progress)). Support: lines 5480-5489 — when activation_type==0 the tanh pipeline registers (slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed, intercept_applied_data_internal) are not assigned in that branch and therefore hold; line 5534 — intercept_applied_data_internal is read only when activation_type==1; line 5461 — out_data exposes it only when enable_activation==1. The rewrite reproduces the "clear" and "load" branches exactly; only the frozen-pipeline (ReLU-mode) branch is not reproduced, and it is observable only if activation_type goes 0→1 without the unit leaving the active state in between. Lines 4838/4913 (activation_type resets to 1 and is only changed by an APB write to REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR) and 5259-5262 (the chain only starts on start_tpu) show the CSR is a configuration register written before a run. Note also that a run held entirely in ReLU mode is still exact: intercept_applied_data_internal is unread then (5534), and the first cycle in which the unit leaves the active state drives the block's reset high, so the block's result is 0 again exactly when the original register is 0.

Converted region — the 16 multiplies at line 5482 (one per lane, 1 instance of activation)

What the original does, per lane i, in the one always block at 5465:

  • branch A (reset || ~enable_activation) and branch C (idle): slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, data_intercept_flopped, intercept_applied_data_internal <= 0;
  • branch B with activation_type==1: S <= data_slope_flopped*inp_data_flopped (8-bit, mod 256), dif <= data_intercept, D <= dif, I <= S + D (mod 256).
    S (slope_applied_data_internal) and D (data_intercept_delayed) are read nowhere except the sum at 5485 (verified by searching the whole file), so the pair can be replaced by the single value S+D, and that value, taken two edges after its operands, is exactly what the block's result holds: result = data_slope_flopped*inp_data_flopped + data_intercept_flopped, all three read at the same instant, product and sum truncated to 8 bits by the slice assignment — the same value mod 256 the original computes. With the block's reset driven by the "clear" condition, all three cases line up: clear at edge e → result 0 after e (original I = 0); load at e after a clear at e-1 → result = 0*0+0 = 0 after e (original S=D=0, so I = 0); load at e and e-1 → result = ds_f*idf+dif of the cycle before e (original I = S+D with exactly those values). Operand widths: both operands are unsigned 8-bit, so the 18/19-bit ports are zero-extended and the 37-bit product never wraps; bx carries the intercept byte as the raw 8-bit pattern, as the original 8-bit add does.

Register stages, lane i:

  • Before: data_slope_flopped / inp_data_flopped / data_intercept_flopped → (stage 1) slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed → (stage 2) intercept_applied_data_internal. Two stages.
  • After: the same three signals → (stage 1) the block's ax/ay/bx input registers → (stage 2) the block's result register. Two stages, same latency and same values. The stages feeding the block (inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped at 5450-5458, and data_intercept_flopped at 5483) are unchanged, so inp_data → out_data is still three register stages and out_data_available / done_activation / cycle_count / the ReLU path are untouched.

Blocks added: 16 (one DSP tile each; the design uses 94 DSP tiles today, so the result stays well under the 132 available). Soft logic removed: the 16 8×8 multiplies, the 16 8-bit adds, and 384 flip-flops (slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, intercept_applied_data_internal, 128 bits each).

Edits for the editor (module activation, lines 5417-5626 — change nothing else in the file, and do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19; instantiate it by name):

  1. Line 5433: delete the declaration of slope_applied_data_internal. Line 5444: delete the declaration of data_intercept_delayed. Line 5434: change intercept_applied_data_internal from reg to wire [\DESIGN_SIZE*\DWIDTH-1:0].
  2. In the always block at 5465, delete only these assignments: 5467, 5468, 5470 (branch A), 5482, 5484, 5485 (branch B's tanh arm), 5522, 5523, 5525 (branch C). Keep everything else exactly as it is, in particular data_intercept_flopped <= 0 (5471, 5526) and data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept[i*8 +: 8] (5483), the ReLU arm (5487), cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_activation_internal, activation_in_progress and in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available (5476). The tanh arm of the for loop then contains only line 5483; keep the if(activation_type==1'b1) ... else ... structure as is.
  3. After the always block (before line 5534), add:
wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress);
genvar gi;
generate
for (gi = 0; gi < `DESIGN_SIZE; gi = gi + 1) begin : act_tanh_dsp
  wire [63:0] blk_result;
  wire [63:0] blk_chainout;
  wire [18:0] blk_scanout;
  mult_add_int_18x19 u_act_mul_add (
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(act_blk_reset),
    .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, data_slope_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}),
    .ay({11'b0, inp_data_flopped[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]}),
    .bx({28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}),
    .chainin(64'b0),
    .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(blk_result),
    .chainout(blk_chainout),
    .scanout(blk_scanout));
  assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = blk_result[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

(16 hand-unrolled instances are acceptable if the generate form gives the flow trouble; the connections must be identical.) chainout/scanout are left dangling — the blocks are not chained, so chainin is tied to 0 as required.

Power-up: at time 0 and through the testbench's reset phase, reset=1 and enable_activation=0, so act_blk_reset is high and every block register is cleared before the first compared cycle; no X can reach out_data.

Candidates left this pass

  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368 … $413 (16 multiplies, module norm): left, correctness/timing. The two design stages (mean_applied_data, then variance_applied_data) match the block's two stages, but the block registers both operands at its entry while the design holds only mean_applied_data in a register at that point: inv_var (5010, from the cfg register at 4889) is read live by the multiply. Mapping it to ay would multiply by the previous cycle's inv_var, which is the original's behaviour only under an assumption that inv_var is stable across a normalization run. That assumption is not needed for the activation rewrite, so norm is not chosen this pass; it is the obvious next candidate if the activation result is favourable.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349 (256 instances, the systolic array's PEs): left, two reasons. (a) Capacity: the block takes a DSP tile alone and qmult has 256 instances in one module, so converting the region needs 256 tiles on a device with 132. (b) Even for a subset, the accumulator in seq_mac (4699-4704: out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg, every cycle) cannot use the block's bx as the accumulator feedback: bx is registered at the block's entry, so the loop would add result from two cycles earlier and split the accumulation into two interleaved sums. Using the block only as the multiplier (a_flopped/b_flopped and mul_out_temp_reg are a 2-stage match) would be behaviourally correct, but it needs one tile per PE and so is out on capacity.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two source changes would open the rest of this design to the block. (1) In norm, register inv_var one stage earlier (a local inv_var_flopped sampled with the same clear/enable as mean_applied_data, used in the product at 5070): the multiply would then take both operands from registers at the same stage and map onto the block with no assumption. (2) In activation, assign the tanh pipeline registers (5482-5485) unconditionally on activation_type rather than only in the tanh arm — the ReLU arm leaves them frozen — which would remove the need for the ASSUMES above. (3) The 256 PE MACs would be the real prize, but they need one DSP tile each; a mode that fits several 8×8 MACs per tile, or a device with more DSP columns, would be required.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the design source and check the plan against it.

APPROVE

Verification notes (checked against tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and sim_reference/):

  • Readers. slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed are declared at 5433/5444, assigned at 5467/5482/5522 and 5470/5484/5525, and read only in the sum at 5485; intercept_applied_data_internal is read only at 5534. So collapsing the two stage‑1 registers into the block's operand registers hides nothing (Visibility holds).
  • Value and latency. Original I(after e) = S(e-1)+D(e-1) = ds_f·idf + dif, all sampled before edge e-1; the block's result(after e) is ax·ay+bx from the same instant. Low 8 bits of the 64‑bit unsigned result equal ((a·b mod 256)+c) mod 256, matching the 8‑bit slice assignments at 5482/5485. Operands are unsigned 8‑bit (DWIDTH=8), zero‑extended into 18/19/36 correctly; DESIGN_SIZE=16 so 16 lanes is right.
  • Reset. act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) is exactly the union of branch A (5466) and branch C (5521), both synchronous, and the clear→load and load→load transitions line up in both directions (including a mid‑run reset pulse).
  • ASSUMES. Needed only because the ReLU arm (5487) freezes the tanh registers while the block keeps loading; cited lines 5480‑5489, 5534, 5461, 4838, 4913, 5259‑5262 all support the text. On the reference stimulus it holds: activation_type resets to 1 (4838) and config_blocks writes 0x3a←1 before each directed run, and the random phase that writes 0x3a with random data (drive file lines 34‑46, reg_addr entry 12) runs with start_tpu cleared, so c_data_available is held 0 by reset | ~start_mat_mul (2305‑2307) and the activation unit is in its cleared branch the whole time — intercept_applied_data_internal is 0 in both versions there.
  • Power‑up. reset=1 for the testbench's 4 reset cycles drives act_blk_reset high, so every block register is loaded before the first compared cycle.
  • Left regions. norm 5070 ×16: the live inv_var (5010, written at 4889) would be registered a cycle early by ay — correct reason, marked not chosen this pass. qmult 4728: 256 instances vs 132 tiles, plus the bx accumulator‑feedback argument against 4697‑4704 — accurate.
  • Required items. All candidate regions accounted for, 16 blocks stated, stage counts before (2) and after (2) given per converted lane. Edit line references (5433/5434/5444, deletions 5467/5468/5470/5482/5484/5485/5522/5523/5525, retentions 5471/5476/5483/5487/5526) are all correct.

1	        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
2	        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
3	        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
4	        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
5	        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
6	        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
7	        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
8	        fill_matrices;
9	        phase = "run1";
10	        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
21	        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
22	`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
23	        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
24	`endif
25	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
26	        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
27	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
28	        phase = "run3";
29	        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
30	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
31	        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
32	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
33	        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
34	        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
35	        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin

39	                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
40	                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
41	                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end

1	    // tpu_like.small.ws top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the design driven as its
2	    // software drives it. Matrices written into the two BRAMs through the external ports (word k of A at
3	    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this
4	    // design's address strides reset to 1), the register block configured over APB, start_tpu written,
5	    // done_tpu polled, the 18 words around address_mat_c read back from BRAM A. A multiply writes its
6	    // results once per reset (the accumulator's start_pooling is cleared by resetn alone, the activation
7	    // unit's done count by reset alone), so every run after the first follows a 4-cycle reset of both.
8	    reg [7:0] MA [0:767]; reg [7:0] MB [0:767];
9	    reg [31:0] rd; reg [127:0] wv; integer polls, nz_c, ii, jj, kk, ph;
10	    reg [7:0] apb_addr; reg apb_write_q; reg [31:0] apb_wdata;   // the random phase's setup-cycle address, direction and write data, held through its access cycle
11	    task fill_matrices; integer q; begin
12	        for (q = 0; q < 768; q = q + 1) begin draw; MA[q] = r32[7:0]; end
13	        for (q = 0; q < 768; q = q + 1) begin draw; MB[q] = r32[7:0]; end
14	    end endtask
15	    task apb_write; input [7:0] a; input [31:0] d; begin
16	        tick; PSEL = 1; PWRITE = 1; PADDR = a; PWDATA = d; PENABLE = 0;
17	        tick; PENABLE = 1;
18	        tick; PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0;
19	        tick;
20	    end endtask
21	    task apb_read; input [7:0] a; begin

29	        tick; bram_we_a_ext = 0;

39	        end

42	            bram_write_b(base_b + 16 * kk, wv);
43	        end
44	    end endtask
45	    task wait_done; input integer maxpolls; begin
46	        polls = 0; rd = 0;
47	        while (rd[31] == 0 && polls < maxpolls) begin apb_read(8'h04); polls = polls + 1; end
48	        if (rd[31]) $display("  t=%0t  done_tpu read as 1 over APB after %0d polls", $time, polls);
49	        else        $display("  t=%0t  done_tpu not read as 1 within %0d polls", $time, maxpolls);
50	    end endtask
51	    task read_c; input [9:0] cbase; input [8*40-1:0] what; begin   // the 18 words from address_mat_c + 16 downward by the stride 16
52	        nz_c = 0;

56	        end
57	        $display("  result %0s: 18 words read back from BRAM A at %0d down to %0d by 16, %0d of 288 bytes nonzero on the reference", what, cbase + 16, cbase - 256, nz_c);
58	    end endtask

60	        apb_write(8'h08, 32'h12); apb_write(8'h0a, 32'h34); apb_write(8'h3a, 32'h1);

prompt
Task

Edit tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiplies in the approved plan: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530. The plan names their shared sum as 'NONE (16 independent lanes; each block sums only its own product with its own intercept term through bx; no chaining)'.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (16 independent lanes; each block sums only its own product with its own intercept term through bx; no chaining)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one mult_add_int_18x19 per lane gi = 0..15 of module activation: clk = clk; reset = (reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)); mode_sigs = 12'b0; ax = {10'b0, data_slope_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; ay = {11'b0, inp_data_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}; chainin = 64'b0; scanin = 19'b0; result[7:0] drives intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: activation_type does not change while the activation unit is processing data, i.e. while (enable_activation=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || activation_in_progress)). Support: lines 5480-5489 — when activation_type==0 the tanh pipeline registers (slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed, intercept_applied_data_internal) are not assigned in that branch and therefore hold; line 5534 — intercept_applied_data_internal is read only when activation_type==1; line 5461 — out_data exposes it only when enable_activation==1. The rewrite reproduces the "clear" and "load" branches exactly; only the frozen-pipeline (ReLU-mode) branch is not reproduced, and it is observable only if activation_type goes 0→1 without the unit leaving the active state in between. Lines 4838/4913 (activation_type resets to 1 and is only changed by an APB write to REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR) and 5259-5262 (the chain only starts on start_tpu) show the CSR is a configuration register written before a run. Note also that a run held entirely in ReLU mode is still exact: intercept_applied_data_internal is unread then (5534), and the first cycle in which the unit leaves the active state drives the block's reset high, so the block's result is 0 again exactly when the original register is 0.

Converted region — the 16 multiplies at line 5482 (one per lane, 1 instance of activation)

What the original does, per lane i, in the one always block at 5465:

  • branch A (reset || ~enable_activation) and branch C (idle): slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, data_intercept_flopped, intercept_applied_data_internal <= 0;
  • branch B with activation_type==1: S <= data_slope_flopped*inp_data_flopped (8-bit, mod 256), dif <= data_intercept, D <= dif, I <= S + D (mod 256).
    S (slope_applied_data_internal) and D (data_intercept_delayed) are read nowhere except the sum at 5485 (verified by searching the whole file), so the pair can be replaced by the single value S+D, and that value, taken two edges after its operands, is exactly what the block's result holds: result = data_slope_flopped*inp_data_flopped + data_intercept_flopped, all three read at the same instant, product and sum truncated to 8 bits by the slice assignment — the same value mod 256 the original computes. With the block's reset driven by the "clear" condition, all three cases line up: clear at edge e → result 0 after e (original I = 0); load at e after a clear at e-1 → result = 0*0+0 = 0 after e (original S=D=0, so I = 0); load at e and e-1 → result = ds_f*idf+dif of the cycle before e (original I = S+D with exactly those values). Operand widths: both operands are unsigned 8-bit, so the 18/19-bit ports are zero-extended and the 37-bit product never wraps; bx carries the intercept byte as the raw 8-bit pattern, as the original 8-bit add does.

Register stages, lane i:

  • Before: data_slope_flopped / inp_data_flopped / data_intercept_flopped → (stage 1) slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed → (stage 2) intercept_applied_data_internal. Two stages.
  • After: the same three signals → (stage 1) the block's ax/ay/bx input registers → (stage 2) the block's result register. Two stages, same latency and same values. The stages feeding the block (inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped at 5450-5458, and data_intercept_flopped at 5483) are unchanged, so inp_data → out_data is still three register stages and out_data_available / done_activation / cycle_count / the ReLU path are untouched.

Blocks added: 16 (one DSP tile each; the design uses 94 DSP tiles today, so the result stays well under the 132 available). Soft logic removed: the 16 8×8 multiplies, the 16 8-bit adds, and 384 flip-flops (slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, intercept_applied_data_internal, 128 bits each).

Edits for the editor (module activation, lines 5417-5626 — change nothing else in the file, and do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19; instantiate it by name):

  1. Line 5433: delete the declaration of slope_applied_data_internal. Line 5444: delete the declaration of data_intercept_delayed. Line 5434: change intercept_applied_data_internal from reg to wire [\DESIGN_SIZE*\DWIDTH-1:0].
  2. In the always block at 5465, delete only these assignments: 5467, 5468, 5470 (branch A), 5482, 5484, 5485 (branch B's tanh arm), 5522, 5523, 5525 (branch C). Keep everything else exactly as it is, in particular data_intercept_flopped <= 0 (5471, 5526) and data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept[i*8 +: 8] (5483), the ReLU arm (5487), cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_activation_internal, activation_in_progress and in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available (5476). The tanh arm of the for loop then contains only line 5483; keep the if(activation_type==1'b1) ... else ... structure as is.
  3. After the always block (before line 5534), add:
wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress);
genvar gi;
generate
for (gi = 0; gi < `DESIGN_SIZE; gi = gi + 1) begin : act_tanh_dsp
  wire [63:0] blk_result;
  wire [63:0] blk_chainout;
  wire [18:0] blk_scanout;
  mult_add_int_18x19 u_act_mul_add (
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(act_blk_reset),
    .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, data_slope_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}),
    .ay({11'b0, inp_data_flopped[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]}),
    .bx({28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[gi*8 +: 8]}),
    .chainin(64'b0),
    .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(blk_result),
    .chainout(blk_chainout),
    .scanout(blk_scanout));
  assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = blk_result[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

(16 hand-unrolled instances are acceptable if the generate form gives the flow trouble; the connections must be identical.) chainout/scanout are left dangling — the blocks are not chained, so chainin is tied to 0 as required.

Power-up: at time 0 and through the testbench's reset phase, reset=1 and enable_activation=0, so act_blk_reset is high and every block register is cleared before the first compared cycle; no X can reach out_data.

Candidates left this pass

  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368 … $413 (16 multiplies, module norm): left, correctness/timing. The two design stages (mean_applied_data, then variance_applied_data) match the block's two stages, but the block registers both operands at its entry while the design holds only mean_applied_data in a register at that point: inv_var (5010, from the cfg register at 4889) is read live by the multiply. Mapping it to ay would multiply by the previous cycle's inv_var, which is the original's behaviour only under an assumption that inv_var is stable across a normalization run. That assumption is not needed for the activation rewrite, so norm is not chosen this pass; it is the obvious next candidate if the activation result is favourable.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349 (256 instances, the systolic array's PEs): left, two reasons. (a) Capacity: the block takes a DSP tile alone and qmult has 256 instances in one module, so converting the region needs 256 tiles on a device with 132. (b) Even for a subset, the accumulator in seq_mac (4699-4704: out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg, every cycle) cannot use the block's bx as the accumulator feedback: bx is registered at the block's entry, so the loop would add result from two cycles earlier and split the accumulation into two interleaved sums. Using the block only as the multiplier (a_flopped/b_flopped and mul_out_temp_reg are a 2-stage match) would be behaviourally correct, but it needs one tile per PE and so is out on capacity.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
===
// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
// inputs).
// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
// X until the first clear.
module mult_add_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
    input [17:0] ax,
    input [18:0] ay,
    input [35:0] bx,
    input [63:0] chainin,
    input [18:0] scanin,
    output reg [63:0] result,

11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
21	        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
22	`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
23	        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
24	`endif
25	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
26	        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
27	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
28	        phase = "run3";
29	        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
30	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
31	        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
32	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
33	        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
34	        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
35	        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
36	            tick; set_data;
37	            case (ph)
38	                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
39	                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
40	                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
41	                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
42	            endcase
43	            ph = (ph + 1) % 4;
44	            draw; bram_we_a_ext = (r32[1:0] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0; bram_we_b_ext = (r32[3:2] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0;
45	            draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % 1024) == 0); resetn = ~reset;
46	        end
47	        PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; bram_we_a_ext = 0; bram_we_b_ext = 0;

3	    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this

  .activation_type(activation_type),
  .enable_activation(enable_activation),
  .in_data_available(pool_out_data_available),

//Interface to BRAM to write the output.
//Ideally, we could remove this flop stage. But then we'd
//have to generate the address for the output BRAM in each
//block that could potentially write the output.
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
---
      done_tpu <= 1'b0;
    end else begin
      case (state)
      `STATE_INIT: begin
        if ((start_tpu == 1'b1) && (done_tpu == 1'b0)) begin
          if (enable_matmul == 1'b1) begin
            start_mat_mul <= 1'b1;
            state <= `STATE_MATMUL;
          end  
        end  
      end
      
      //start_mat_mul is kinda used as a reset in some logic
      //inside the matmul unit. So, we can't make it 0 right away after
      //asserting it.
      `STATE_MATMUL: begin
        if (done_mat_mul == 1'b1) begin
            start_mat_mul <= 1'b0;
            if(save_output_to_accum) begin
              state <= `STATE_DONE;
            end
            else if (enable_norm) begin
              state <= `STATE_NORM;
            end 
            else if (enable_pool) begin
              state <= `STATE_POOL;
            end
            else if (enable_activation) begin
              state <= `STATE_ACTIVATION;
            end
            else begin
              state <= `STATE_DONE;
            end  
        end 
        else begin
          start_mat_mul <= 1'b1;	      
        end
      end      
      
      `STATE_NORM: begin                 
        if (done_norm == 1'b1) begin
          if (enable_pool) begin
            state <= `STATE_POOL;
          end
          else if (enable_activation) begin
            state <= `STATE_ACTIVATION;
          end
          else begin
            state <= `STATE_DONE;
          end
        end
      end

      `STATE_POOL: begin                 
        if (done_pool == 1'b1) begin
          if (enable_activation) begin
            state <= `STATE_ACTIVATION;
          end
          else begin
            state <= `STATE_DONE;
          end
        end
      end

      `STATE_ACTIVATION: begin                 
        if (done_activation == 1'b1) begin
