The plan

I'll read the source before planning.

REGIONS: norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (16 independent lanes; each block holds one lane's stage-1 value in its own operand register and its own product in result; bx = 0, no chaining)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one mult_add_int_18x19 per lane gn = 0..15 of module norm: clk = clk; reset = (reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress)); mode_sigs = 12'b0; ax = {10'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[gn*8 +: 8])} (the 8-bit difference, truncated before the zero-extension — this operand register is the old mean_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]); ay = {11'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1)}; bx = 36'b0; chainin = 64'b0; scanin = 19'b0; result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask (= validity_mask_a_rows) do not change from one cycle to the next while the norm unit is active, i.e. while (enable_norm=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || norm_in_progress)). Support: 5007-5010 and 5894-5906 — both come straight from the cfg block's registers (inv_var at 4764/4823, validity_mask_a_rows at 4769/4829) and are changed only by a well-formed APB write (4874-4883 and the REG_INV_VAR_ADDR / REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR cases of the same case), i.e. they are configuration written before a run; 5059 — the unit only becomes active once the matmul produces matmul_c_data_available (5898), which only follows start_tpu (4884-4885). The original reads mean and the mask at edge k for stage 1 and reads inv_var and the mask again at edge k+1 for stage 2; the block reads all of them at edge k. The two agree exactly whenever the pair is stable across those two edges. mean needs no assumption (it is used only in stage 1, at the same edge k as before).

Converted region — the 16 multiplies at line 5070 (one per lane, 1 instance of norm)

What the original does, per lane i, in the one always block at 5049:

  • branch A (reset || ~enable_norm) and branch C (idle): mean_applied_data <= 0; variance_applied_data <= 0;
  • branch B (active), mask bit 1: M[i] <= inp_data[i] - mean (8-bit, mod 256) and V[i] <= M[i] * inv_var (8-bit, mod 256);
  • branch B, mask bit 0: M[i] <= inp_data[i] and V[i] <= M[i].

mean_applied_data is read nowhere except those stage-2 expressions (5070 and 5074 — the only readers in the file besides its own clears at 5051/5098 and its declaration at 5023), so it may become the block's ax operand register; variance_applied_data is read only at 5107 (assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;), so it may become the block's result. Visibility therefore holds: nothing outside the block reads either value.

Value match: ax carries the 8-bit stage-1 value (the subtraction truncated to 8 bits before the zero-extension, so the block's unsigned 18-bit operand is exactly the bit pattern the original 8-bit register holds), ay carries inv_var for a valid lane and 1 for an invalid one, so result = ax*ay (37 bits, no wrap) and result[7:0] is (M[i]*inv_var) mod 256 for a valid lane and M[i] for an invalid one — the original value in both cases. bx and chainin are tied to 0, so no term is added that the original does not have, and no term of the original is dropped: the mean subtraction stays outside, in the same soft logic the design already has, feeding ax.

Timing/reset match, edge by edge, with the block's reset driven by the clear condition of branches A and C:

  • clear at edge k: block clears ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, chainin_reg and result → result = 0 after k, matching V <= 0; the original's M <= 0 is matched by ax_reg = 0.
  • active at k, cleared at k-1: result after k = ax_reg*ay_reg from k-1 = 0*0 = 0, and the original gives V <= M(=0) * inv_var = 0 (or V <= M(=0) for an invalid lane) = 0. Match.
  • active at k and k+1: result after k+1 = (value loaded into ax at k) * (value loaded into ay at k) = the original's V after k+1 under the ASSUMES above.
    Power-up: the cfg registers come up with enable_norm = 0 and the testbench holds reset high for its first 4 cycles, so the block's reset is high at the first edges and every block register is defined well before the first compared cycle; no X can reach out_data.

Register stages, lane i:

  • Before: inp_data → (stage 1) mean_applied_data → (stage 2) variance_applied_data. Two stages.
  • After: inp_data → (stage 1) the block's ax/ay input registers → (stage 2) the block's result. Two stages, same latency, same values. cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped and inp_data_flopped are untouched, so out_data_available, done_norm and the disabled-block bypass (5031-5033) are unchanged.

Blocks added: 16 (one DSP tile each; 94 tiles are used today and 16 soft multiplies leave the packed pool, so the design stays far inside the 132 tiles). Soft logic removed: the 16 8×8 multiplies and 256 flip-flops (mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data, 128 bits each); added: the 16 8-bit 2:1 muxes that build ay.

Why this region, after the first pass: the measured critical path of pass 1 is reg → $mul~1965.b[0] → $mul~1965.out[7] → reg, with 2.045 ns of wire on the way into the multiply's b operand and the sink at output bit 7. In mean_applied_data * inv_var the b operand is inv_var, which is driven from the cfg block far away and is the only multiply operand in this design that is not a register local to its own module, and the product is truncated to 8 bits, so bit 7 is its top used bit. In a PE, qmult(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped)) (4689, 4723-4728) has both operands in the PE's own registers and uses all 16 product bits. The rewrite puts that long inv_var route in front of the block's input register (0.060 + wire + 0.019 setup ≈ 2.1 ns) and leaves 2.219 ns inside the block, so the path is cut in two. Downstream nothing lengthens: variance_applied_data was a flip-flop output and becomes the block's result register output, the same 0.060 ns clock-to-Q into the same pool/activation logic.

Edits for the editor (module norm, lines 5007-5109 of the unmodified source — change nothing else in the file; in particular do not apply the first pass's activation edit, and do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19, instantiate it by name):

  1. Line 5023: delete the declaration of mean_applied_data. Line 5024: change variance_applied_data from reg to wire [\DESIGN_SIZE*\DWIDTH-1:0]. Line 5048: delete reg [31:0] i; (its only use is the loop deleted below).
  2. In the always block at 5049: delete lines 5051 and 5052 (branch A's two clears), delete the whole for loop at 5067-5076 (its body contains only the two assignments being replaced), and delete lines 5098 and 5099 (branch C's two clears). Keep everything else exactly as it is: out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available and inp_data_flopped <= inp_data (5053-5058, 5060, 5080-5095, 5100-5103).
  3. Between the end of the always block (5105) and assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data; (5107), add:
//The mean-applied stage and the variance multiply are mapped onto one mult_add_int_18x19 per
//lane: the block's ax register holds what mean_applied_data held, result holds variance_applied_data.
wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
genvar gn;
generate
for (gn = 0; gn < `DESIGN_SIZE; gn = gn + 1) begin : norm_dsp
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_nxt =
      validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean)
                        : inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH];
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] scale_nxt = validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
  wire [63:0] blk_result;
  wire [63:0] blk_chainout;
  wire [18:0] blk_scanout;
  mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mul_add (
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(norm_blk_reset),
    .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, mean_applied_nxt}),
    .ay({11'b0, scale_nxt}),
    .bx(36'b0),
    .chainin(64'b0),
    .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(blk_result),
    .chainout(blk_chainout),
    .scanout(blk_scanout));
  assign variance_applied_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = blk_result[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

(16 hand-unrolled instances are acceptable if the generate form gives the flow trouble; the connections must be identical. mean_applied_nxt must be declared 8 bits wide so the subtraction truncates to 8 bits before the zero-extension to ax.) chainout/scanout are left dangling — the blocks are not chained, so chainin is tied to 0 as required, and nothing reads chainout.

Candidates left this pass

  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 … $530 (16 multiplies, module activation): left, not chosen this pass. The pass-1 rewrite of them is correct (it passed the reference simulation), but it was measured at +2.08% area and D/D0 = 1.000: the 16 paths it removed were not the critical ones, and each block costs a whole DSP tile where four 8×8 multiplies otherwise share one. Converting them again would only add that area to this pass. It stays available if the norm result is favourable and a second region is worth its tile cost.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349 (256 instances, the systolic array's PEs — 16×16 processing_elements at 4112 ff., each with one seq_mac): left, a capacity reason. The block takes a DSP tile alone and the array needs one per PE, i.e. 256 tiles on a 132-tile device, so the region cannot be converted whole; a subset gains nothing, because the uncut PE multiply paths keep the same period. (Separately, the accumulator at 4697-4704 cannot use bx as its feedback: bx is registered at the block's entry, so the loop would add result from two cycles earlier and split the accumulation into two interleaved sums; the block would have to serve as the multiplier only, which is what makes it one tile per PE.)

DESIGN_CHANGE: (1) The period of this design is set by reg → 8×8 multiply → reg paths, 256 of which are the PE MACs; with one DSP tile per block none of them can move onto this block (256 needed, 132 available). A mode that fits several 8×8 multiply-adds in one tile, or more DSP columns, is what would let the array use it and is the only change that can move this design's critical path. (2) In norm, registering inv_var and validity_mask one stage earlier (a local copy sampled with the same clear/enable as mean_applied_data, used at 5070-5074) would make the block's entry-registered operands exact and remove the need for the ASSUMES above. (3) In activation, assigning the tanh pipeline registers (5482-5485) unconditionally rather than only in the activation_type==1 arm would likewise remove the assumption that pass 1 needed there.

Reference simulation

A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

APPROVE

Checks performed against tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and sim_reference/:

Visibility. mean_applied_data is declared at 5023 and read only at 5070 and 5074 (plus its own clears at 5051/5098) — nothing outside the module or the always block reads it, so it may become the block's ax register. variance_applied_data (5024) is read only at 5107 (assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;), so it may become result. Grep confirms no other readers in the file.

Reset drive. The always block at 5049 has exactly three arms: clear at 5050 (reset || ~enable_norm), load at 5059 (in_data_available || norm_in_progress), clear at 5097. norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress) is the exact complement of the load arm, and the load arm assigns both registers unconditionally every active cycle (the for body at 5067–5076 has no freeze branch, unlike activation's activation_type arm). Clear/first-load/steady-state edges all line up as the plan tabulates, including result = 0·0 = 0 on the first active edge after a clear, which matches V <= M(=0)*inv_var.

Terms and widths. DWIDTH=8, DESIGN_SIZE=MASK_WIDTH=16. ax = {10'b0, 8-bit} = 18, ay = {11'b0, 8-bit} = 19, bx = 36'b0, chainin = 64'b0, scanin = 19'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0; every input port connected by name, chainout/scanout dangling and unread. The subtraction is declared 8 bits wide so it truncates before zero-extension, matching the 8-bit part-select assignment at 5069; ay=1 for a masked-off lane reproduces the pass-through at 5074; the 37-bit product cannot wrap and result[7:0] is the original mod-256 value.

ASSUMES. The assumption also holds on the reference stimulus: config_blocks (tasks 59-62) writes 0x08/0x0a before apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1), and wait_done only issues APB reads, so neither register changes during runs 2 and 3; in the random phase the drive file remaps PADDR==8'h04 to 8'h00 (drive line 38), so start_tpu is never set, the matmul stays idle and norm stays in its clear arm while 0x0a/0x20 are written randomly. mean correctly needs no assumption (same-edge use). reset_pattern/impulse_probe are defined but not called in the tb's main sequence (tb 208-218).

Power-up. Block registers are X until the first clear; the tb holds reset high for RESET_CYCLES and compares from the first cycle after release (tb 210-212), so result is defined before the first compared cycle.

Edits. Line numbers for the deletions (5023, 5024, 5048, 5051-5052, 5067-5076, 5098-5099) match the source; i (5048) is used only by the deleted loop; in_data_available_flopped/inp_data_flopped (5057-5058) and the disabled-block bypass (5031-5033) are preserved, so out_data_available, done_norm and the bypass path are unchanged. 5063	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).

5075	            end
5076	        end

5079	        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.

1	        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
2	        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
3	        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
4	        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
5	        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
6	        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
7	        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
8	        fill_matrices;
9	        phase = "run1";
10	        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
21	        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
22	`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
23	        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
24	`endif
25	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
26	        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
27	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
28	        phase = "run3";
29	        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
30	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
31	        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
32	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
33	        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
34	        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
35	        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
36	            tick; set_data;
37	            case (ph)
38	                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
39	                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
40	                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
41	                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
42	            endcase
43	            ph = (ph + 1) % 4;
44	            draw; bram_we_a_ext = (r32[1:0] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0; bram_we_b_ext = (r32[3:2] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0;
45	            draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % 1024) == 0); resetn = ~reset;
46	        end
47	        PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; bram_we_a_ext = 0; bram_we_b_ext = 0;

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule
57

prompt
Task

Edit tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiplies in the approved plan: norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413. The plan names their shared sum as 'NONE (16 independent lanes; each block holds one lane's stage-1 value in its own operand register and its own product in result; bx = 0, no chaining)'.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the source before planning.

REGIONS: norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (16 independent lanes; each block holds one lane's stage-1 value in its own operand register and its own product in result; bx = 0, no chaining)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one mult_add_int_18x19 per lane gn = 0..15 of module norm: clk = clk; reset = (reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress)); mode_sigs = 12'b0; ax = {10'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[gn*8 +: 8])} (the 8-bit difference, truncated before the zero-extension — this operand register is the old mean_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]); ay = {11'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1)}; bx = 36'b0; chainin = 64'b0; scanin = 19'b0; result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask (= validity_mask_a_rows) do not change from one cycle to the next while the norm unit is active, i.e. while (enable_norm=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || norm_in_progress)). Support: 5007-5010 and 5894-5906 — both come straight from the cfg block's registers (inv_var at 4764/4823, validity_mask_a_rows at 4769/4829) and are changed only by a well-formed APB write (4874-4883 and the REG_INV_VAR_ADDR / REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR cases of the same case), i.e. they are configuration written before a run; 5059 — the unit only becomes active once the matmul produces matmul_c_data_available (5898), which only follows start_tpu (4884-4885). The original reads mean and the mask at edge k for stage 1 and reads inv_var and the mask again at edge k+1 for stage 2; the block reads all of them at edge k. The two agree exactly whenever the pair is stable across those two edges. mean needs no assumption (it is used only in stage 1, at the same edge k as before).

Converted region — the 16 multiplies at line 5070 (one per lane, 1 instance of norm)

What the original does, per lane i, in the one always block at 5049:

  • branch A (reset || ~enable_norm) and branch C (idle): mean_applied_data <= 0; variance_applied_data <= 0;
  • branch B (active), mask bit 1: M[i] <= inp_data[i] - mean (8-bit, mod 256) and V[i] <= M[i] * inv_var (8-bit, mod 256);
  • branch B, mask bit 0: M[i] <= inp_data[i] and V[i] <= M[i].

mean_applied_data is read nowhere except those stage-2 expressions (5070 and 5074 — the only readers in the file besides its own clears at 5051/5098 and its declaration at 5023), so it may become the block's ax operand register; variance_applied_data is read only at 5107 (assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;), so it may become the block's result. Visibility therefore holds: nothing outside the block reads either value.

Value match: ax carries the 8-bit stage-1 value (the subtraction truncated to 8 bits before the zero-extension, so the block's unsigned 18-bit operand is exactly the bit pattern the original 8-bit register holds), ay carries inv_var for a valid lane and 1 for an invalid one, so result = ax*ay (37 bits, no wrap) and result[7:0] is (M[i]*inv_var) mod 256 for a valid lane and M[i] for an invalid one — the original value in both cases. bx and chainin are tied to 0, so no term is added that the original does not have, and no term of the original is dropped: the mean subtraction stays outside, in the same soft logic the design already has, feeding ax.

Timing/reset match, edge by edge, with the block's reset driven by the clear condition of branches A and C:

  • clear at edge k: block clears ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, chainin_reg and result → result = 0 after k, matching V <= 0; the original's M <= 0 is matched by ax_reg = 0.
  • active at k, cleared at k-1: result after k = ax_reg*ay_reg from k-1 = 0*0 = 0, and the original gives V <= M(=0) * inv_var = 0 (or V <= M(=0) for an invalid lane) = 0. Match.
  • active at k and k+1: result after k+1 = (value loaded into ax at k) * (value loaded into ay at k) = the original's V after k+1 under the ASSUMES above.
    Power-up: the cfg registers come up with enable_norm = 0 and the testbench holds reset high for its first 4 cycles, so the block's reset is high at the first edges and every block register is defined well before the first compared cycle; no X can reach out_data.

Register stages, lane i:

  • Before: inp_data → (stage 1) mean_applied_data → (stage 2) variance_applied_data. Two stages.
  • After: inp_data → (stage 1) the block's ax/ay input registers → (stage 2) the block's result. Two stages, same latency, same values. cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped and inp_data_flopped are untouched, so out_data_available, done_norm and the disabled-block bypass (5031-5033) are unchanged.

Blocks added: 16 (one DSP tile each; 94 tiles are used today and 16 soft multiplies leave the packed pool, so the design stays far inside the 132 tiles). Soft logic removed: the 16 8×8 multiplies and 256 flip-flops (mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data, 128 bits each); added: the 16 8-bit 2:1 muxes that build ay.

Why this region, after the first pass: the measured critical path of pass 1 is reg → $mul~1965.b[0] → $mul~1965.out[7] → reg, with 2.045 ns of wire on the way into the multiply's b operand and the sink at output bit 7. In mean_applied_data * inv_var the b operand is inv_var, which is driven from the cfg block far away and is the only multiply operand in this design that is not a register local to its own module, and the product is truncated to 8 bits, so bit 7 is its top used bit. In a PE, qmult(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped)) (4689, 4723-4728) has both operands in the PE's own registers and uses all 16 product bits. The rewrite puts that long inv_var route in front of the block's input register (0.060 + wire + 0.019 setup ≈ 2.1 ns) and leaves 2.219 ns inside the block, so the path is cut in two. Downstream nothing lengthens: variance_applied_data was a flip-flop output and becomes the block's result register output, the same 0.060 ns clock-to-Q into the same pool/activation logic.

Edits for the editor (module norm, lines 5007-5109 of the unmodified source — change nothing else in the file; in particular do not apply the first pass's activation edit, and do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19, instantiate it by name):

  1. Line 5023: delete the declaration of mean_applied_data. Line 5024: change variance_applied_data from reg to wire [\DESIGN_SIZE*\DWIDTH-1:0]. Line 5048: delete reg [31:0] i; (its only use is the loop deleted below).
  2. In the always block at 5049: delete lines 5051 and 5052 (branch A's two clears), delete the whole for loop at 5067-5076 (its body contains only the two assignments being replaced), and delete lines 5098 and 5099 (branch C's two clears). Keep everything else exactly as it is: out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available and inp_data_flopped <= inp_data (5053-5058, 5060, 5080-5095, 5100-5103).
  3. Between the end of the always block (5105) and assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data; (5107), add:
//The mean-applied stage and the variance multiply are mapped onto one mult_add_int_18x19 per
//lane: the block's ax register holds what mean_applied_data held, result holds variance_applied_data.
wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
genvar gn;
generate
for (gn = 0; gn < `DESIGN_SIZE; gn = gn + 1) begin : norm_dsp
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_nxt =
      validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean)
                        : inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH];
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] scale_nxt = validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
  wire [63:0] blk_result;
  wire [63:0] blk_chainout;
  wire [18:0] blk_scanout;
  mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mul_add (
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(norm_blk_reset),
    .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, mean_applied_nxt}),
    .ay({11'b0, scale_nxt}),
    .bx(36'b0),
    .chainin(64'b0),
    .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(blk_result),
    .chainout(blk_chainout),
    .scanout(blk_scanout));
  assign variance_applied_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = blk_result[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

(16 hand-unrolled instances are acceptable if the generate form gives the flow trouble; the connections must be identical. mean_applied_nxt must be declared 8 bits wide so the subtraction truncates to 8 bits before the zero-extension to ax.) chainout/scanout are left dangling — the blocks are not chained, so chainin is tied to 0 as required, and nothing reads chainout.

Candidates left this pass

  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 … $530 (16 multiplies, module activation): left, not chosen this pass. The pass-1 rewrite of them is correct (it passed the reference simulation), but it was measured at +2.08% area and D/D0 = 1.000: the 16 paths it removed were not the critical ones, and each block costs a whole DSP tile where four 8×8 multiplies otherwise share one. Converting them again would only add that area to this pass. It stays available if the norm result is favourable and a second region is worth its tile cost.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349 (256 instances, the systolic array's PEs — 16×16 processing_elements at 4112 ff., each with one seq_mac): left, a capacity reason. The block takes a DSP tile alone and the array needs one per PE, i.e. 256 tiles on a 132-tile device, so the region cannot be converted whole; a subset gains nothing, because the uncut PE multiply paths keep the same period. (Separately, the accumulator at 4697-4704 cannot use bx as its feedback: bx is registered at the block's entry, so the loop would add result from two cycles earlier and split the accumulation into two interleaved sums; the block would have to serve as the multiplier only, which is what makes it one tile per PE.)
Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

response

I'll start by reading the relevant region of the design.

Baseline passes. Now applying the plan's edits to module norm.

The model matches the contract the plan assumed. check.sh passes with 16 mult_add_int_18x19 instances.

What I changed — module norm only (one region, the 16 multiplies at the old line 5070)

Declarations (old 5023–5024, 5048) — deleted reg mean_applied_data (it becomes the block's ax input register), changed variance_applied_data from reg to wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] (it becomes the blocks' result), and deleted reg [31:0] i;, whose only use was the deleted loop.

Always block at the old 5049 — removed the two clears of mean_applied_data / variance_applied_data in the reset/disabled branch (old 5051–5052) and in the idle branch (old 5098–5099), and removed the whole for loop (old 5067–5076), whose body held only the two replaced assignments. Everything else in the block is byte-for-byte unchanged: out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped, inp_data_flopped, and the cycle_count==2 / cycle_count==DESIGN_SIZE+1 logic.

New hardware, between the always block and assign out_data_internal (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5090-5117) — a generate loop of 16 mult_add_int_18x19 instances, one per lane, instantiated by name with every input port connected as the plan specifies:

  • clk = clk; reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress) — the exact union of the original's two clearing branches;
  • ax = {10'b0, mean_applied_nxt} with mean_applied_nxt declared 8 bits wide, so the mean subtraction truncates mod 256 before the zero-extension; the mean subtract stays in soft logic outside the block;
  • ay = {11'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1)};
  • bx = 36'b0, chainin = 64'b0 (not chained), scanin = 19'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0;
  • result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout are left unread.

Nothing outside module norm was touched; in particular the activation multiplies were left as-is.

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask are stable from one cycle to the next while the norm unit is active (enable_norm=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || norm_in_progress)). The original samples mean and the mask at edge k for stage 1 and samples inv_var and the mask again at edge k+1 for stage 2, whereas the block samples all of them at edge k. 1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
3	# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
4	# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
5	# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
6	# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
7	# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
8	# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
9	# of the target block's name fails this check.
10	# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
11	# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
12	# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
13	# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
14	set -u
15	f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
16	here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
17	lib=""
18	[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
19	[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
20	target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
21	if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
22	  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
23	  exit 1
24	fi
25	out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
26	rc=$?
27	if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
28	  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
29	  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
30	  echo "$out" | tail -10
31	  exit 1
32	fi
33	echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
34	echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
35	echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
36	                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
37	                   on {on=0}
38	                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
39	  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Bash completed cd /work/sim_reference && cat mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "command": "cd /work/sim_reference && cat mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
// inputs).
// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
// X until the first clear.
module mult_add_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
    input [17:0] ax,
    input [18:0] ay,
    input [35:0] bx,
    input [63:0] chainin,
    input [18:0] scanin,
    output reg [63:0] result,
    output [63:0] chainout,
    output [18:0] scanout
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            result <= 0;
            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
        end
    end

    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
    assign scanout  = ay_reg;

endmodule
Call 8 · acceptance reviewer · ACCEPT · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 20 · out 9002 · reasoning 6328 · cache read 228991 · cache write 28903 · 126.0 s

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll read the source before planning.

REGIONS: norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (16 independent lanes; each block holds one lane's stage-1 value in its own operand register and its own product in result; bx = 0, no chaining)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one mult_add_int_18x19 per lane gn = 0..15 of module norm: clk = clk; reset = (reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress)); mode_sigs = 12'b0; ax = {10'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[gn*8 +: 8])} (the 8-bit difference, truncated before the zero-extension — this operand register is the old mean_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]); ay = {11'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1)}; bx = 36'b0; chainin = 64'b0; scanin = 19'b0; result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask (= validity_mask_a_rows) do not change from one cycle to the next while the norm unit is active, i.e. while (enable_norm=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || norm_in_progress)). Support: 5007-5010 and 5894-5906 — both come straight from the cfg block's registers (inv_var at 4764/4823, validity_mask_a_rows at 4769/4829) and are changed only by a well-formed APB write (4874-4883 and the REG_INV_VAR_ADDR / REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR cases of the same case), i.e. they are configuration written before a run; 5059 — the unit only becomes active once the matmul produces matmul_c_data_available (5898), which only follows start_tpu (4884-4885). The original reads mean and the mask at edge k for stage 1 and reads inv_var and the mask again at edge k+1 for stage 2; the block reads all of them at edge k. The two agree exactly whenever the pair is stable across those two edges. mean needs no assumption (it is used only in stage 1, at the same edge k as before).

Converted region — the 16 multiplies at line 5070 (one per lane, 1 instance of norm)

What the original does, per lane i, in the one always block at 5049:

  • branch A (reset || ~enable_norm) and branch C (idle): mean_applied_data <= 0; variance_applied_data <= 0;
  • branch B (active), mask bit 1: M[i] <= inp_data[i] - mean (8-bit, mod 256) and V[i] <= M[i] * inv_var (8-bit, mod 256);
  • branch B, mask bit 0: M[i] <= inp_data[i] and V[i] <= M[i].

mean_applied_data is read nowhere except those stage-2 expressions (5070 and 5074 — the only readers in the file besides its own clears at 5051/5098 and its declaration at 5023), so it may become the block's ax operand register; variance_applied_data is read only at 5107 (assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;), so it may become the block's result. Visibility therefore holds: nothing outside the block reads either value.

Value match: ax carries the 8-bit stage-1 value (the subtraction truncated to 8 bits before the zero-extension, so the block's unsigned 18-bit operand is exactly the bit pattern the original 8-bit register holds), ay carries inv_var for a valid lane and 1 for an invalid one, so result = ax*ay (37 bits, no wrap) and result[7:0] is (M[i]*inv_var) mod 256 for a valid lane and M[i] for an invalid one — the original value in both cases. bx and chainin are tied to 0, so no term is added that the original does not have, and no term of the original is dropped: the mean subtraction stays outside, in the same soft logic the design already has, feeding ax.

Timing/reset match, edge by edge, with the block's reset driven by the clear condition of branches A and C:

  • clear at edge k: block clears ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, chainin_reg and result → result = 0 after k, matching V <= 0; the original's M <= 0 is matched by ax_reg = 0.
  • active at k, cleared at k-1: result after k = ax_reg*ay_reg from k-1 = 0*0 = 0, and the original gives V <= M(=0) * inv_var = 0 (or V <= M(=0) for an invalid lane) = 0. Match.
  • active at k and k+1: result after k+1 = (value loaded into ax at k) * (value loaded into ay at k) = the original's V after k+1 under the ASSUMES above.
    Power-up: the cfg registers come up with enable_norm = 0 and the testbench holds reset high for its first 4 cycles, so the block's reset is high at the first edges and every block register is defined well before the first compared cycle; no X can reach out_data.

Register stages, lane i:

  • Before: inp_data → (stage 1) mean_applied_data → (stage 2) variance_applied_data. Two stages.
  • After: inp_data → (stage 1) the block's ax/ay input registers → (stage 2) the block's result. Two stages, same latency, same values. cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped and inp_data_flopped are untouched, so out_data_available, done_norm and the disabled-block bypass (5031-5033) are unchanged.

Blocks added: 16 (one DSP tile each; 94 tiles are used today and 16 soft multiplies leave the packed pool, so the design stays far inside the 132 tiles). Soft logic removed: the 16 8×8 multiplies and 256 flip-flops (mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data, 128 bits each); added: the 16 8-bit 2:1 muxes that build ay.

Why this region, after the first pass: the measured critical path of pass 1 is reg → $mul~1965.b[0] → $mul~1965.out[7] → reg, with 2.045 ns of wire on the way into the multiply's b operand and the sink at output bit 7. In mean_applied_data * inv_var the b operand is inv_var, which is driven from the cfg block far away and is the only multiply operand in this design that is not a register local to its own module, and the product is truncated to 8 bits, so bit 7 is its top used bit. In a PE, qmult(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped)) (4689, 4723-4728) has both operands in the PE's own registers and uses all 16 product bits. The rewrite puts that long inv_var route in front of the block's input register (0.060 + wire + 0.019 setup ≈ 2.1 ns) and leaves 2.219 ns inside the block, so the path is cut in two. Downstream nothing lengthens: variance_applied_data was a flip-flop output and becomes the block's result register output, the same 0.060 ns clock-to-Q into the same pool/activation logic.

Edits for the editor (module norm, lines 5007-5109 of the unmodified source — change nothing else in the file; in particular do not apply the first pass's activation edit, and do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19, instantiate it by name):

  1. Line 5023: delete the declaration of mean_applied_data. Line 5024: change variance_applied_data from reg to wire [\DESIGN_SIZE*\DWIDTH-1:0]. Line 5048: delete reg [31:0] i; (its only use is the loop deleted below).
  2. In the always block at 5049: delete lines 5051 and 5052 (branch A's two clears), delete the whole for loop at 5067-5076 (its body contains only the two assignments being replaced), and delete lines 5098 and 5099 (branch C's two clears). Keep everything else exactly as it is: out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available and inp_data_flopped <= inp_data (5053-5058, 5060, 5080-5095, 5100-5103).
  3. Between the end of the always block (5105) and assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data; (5107), add:
//The mean-applied stage and the variance multiply are mapped onto one mult_add_int_18x19 per
//lane: the block's ax register holds what mean_applied_data held, result holds variance_applied_data.
wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
genvar gn;
generate
for (gn = 0; gn < `DESIGN_SIZE; gn = gn + 1) begin : norm_dsp
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_nxt =
      validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean)
                        : inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH];
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] scale_nxt = validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
  wire [63:0] blk_result;
  wire [63:0] blk_chainout;
  wire [18:0] blk_scanout;
  mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mul_add (
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(norm_blk_reset),
    .mode_sigs(12'b0),

    .ay({11'b0, scale_nxt}),

(16 hand-unrolled instances are acceptable if the generate form gives the flow trouble; the connections must be identical. mean_applied_nxt must be declared 8 bits wide so the subtraction truncates to 8 bits before the zero-extension to ax.) chainout/scanout are left dangling — the blocks are not chained, so chainin is tied to 0 as required, and nothing reads chainout.

Candidates left this pass

  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 … $530 (16 multiplies, module activation): left, not chosen this pass. The pass-1 rewrite of them is correct (it passed the reference simulation), but it was measured at +2.08% area and D/D0 = 1.000: the 16 paths it removed were not the critical ones, and each block costs a whole DSP tile where four 8×8 multiplies otherwise share one. Converting them again would only add that area to this pass. It stays available if the norm result is favourable and a second region is worth its tile cost.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349 (256 instances, the systolic array's PEs — 16×16 processing_elements at 4112 ff., each with one seq_mac): left, a capacity reason. The block takes a DSP tile alone and the array needs one per PE, i.e. 256 tiles on a 132-tile device, so the region cannot be converted whole; a subset gains nothing, because the uncut PE multiply paths keep the same period. (Separately, the accumulator at 4697-4704 cannot use bx as its feedback: bx is registered at the block's entry, so the loop would add result from two cycles earlier and split the accumulation into two interleaved sums; the block would have to serve as the multiplier only, which is what makes it one tile per PE.)

DESIGN_CHANGE: (1) The period of this design is set by reg → 8×8 multiply → reg paths, 256 of which are the PE MACs; with one DSP tile per block none of them can move onto this block (256 needed, 132 available). A mode that fits several 8×8 multiply-adds in one tile, or more DSP columns, is what would let the array use it and is the only change that can move this design's critical path. (2) In norm, registering inv_var and validity_mask one stage earlier (a local copy sampled with the same clear/enable as mean_applied_data, used at 5070-5074) would make the block's entry-registered operands exact and remove the need for the ASSUMES above. (3) In activation, assigning the tanh pipeline registers (5482-5485) unconditionally rather than only in the activation_type==1 arm would likewise remove the assumption that pass 1 needed there.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance — The edit touches only module norm (lines 5021–5119). It does exactly the three steps the plan names: removed the mean_applied_data declaration and made variance_applied_data a wire (5023), removed reg [31:0] i; (its only use was the deleted loop; the genvar i at 5148 is in a different module), deleted the two clears in branch A, the for loop, and the two clears in branch C, and added the generate block at 5090–5117 with the connections given in the plan verbatim. activation (5429–5638) is untouched — the pass‑1 tanh edit was correctly not applied — and mult_add_int_18x19 appears only as an instantiation, never as a definition.

Ports — All eight inputs connected by name: clk, reset(norm_blk_reset), mode_sigs(12'b0), ax({10'b0,mean_applied_nxt}), ay({11'b0,scale_nxt}), bx(36'b0), chainin(64'b0), scanin(19'b0). chainin is a constant (legal driver), scanin/mode_sigs tied to 0 as required. result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]; chainout/scanout go to local wires nothing reads, so chainout reaches no general routing. 16 lanes × 1 tile = 16 of 132 DSP tiles.

Values — mean_applied_nxt is declared 8 bits, so the subtraction truncates mod 256 before the zero‑extension to ax, exactly the old 8‑bit mean_applied_data pattern; ay = inv_var for a valid lane, 8'd1 for an invalid one, so result[7:0] = (M*inv_var) mod 256 and M respectively — both original values. bx/chainin = 0 add no term; the mean subtraction stays in soft logic, so no term is dropped.

Visibility — mean_applied_data has no remaining reference anywhere in the file, and variance_applied_data is read only at 5119 (out_data_internal). Nothing outside the block reads either value.

Registers/reset — norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress) is the exact complement of the original's active branch, so the block clears ax/ay/bx/chainin and result on precisely the edges where the original cleared M and V. The first active edge after a clear gives result = 0*0 = 0, matching V <= M(=0)*inv_var. Latency is unchanged (2 stages, same as inp_data → M → V); cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress and the bypass at 5030–5032 are untouched. At power‑up reset is high, so norm_blk_reset is 1 regardless of the X on norm_in_progress/enable_norm, and every block register is defined from the first edge — consistent with x_count: 0.

ASSUMES — The plan's assumption (that inv_var and validity_mask_a_rows are stable across the two edges while the unit is active) is the only gap between the original's stage‑2 read at edge k+1 and the block's entry read at edge k. It is supported: both come from cfg registers (5826/5831, 5909/5914 → 4769/4829/4894) changed only by an APB write, and the unit activates only on matmul_c_data_available (5910). The testbench stimulus does not depend on it — 0 mismatches over 4577 cycles with one mid‑run reset.

The two verdict: "error" diagnostics in sim_result.json are "nofile" row probes for systolic_pe_matrix and activation (sim_run.log:27–28), not comparison failures; both report 0 mismatches and neither is the verdict.

5077	        end
5078	        else begin
5079	            norm_in_progress <= 1;
5080	        end
5081	    end

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

No rewrite
  • REGIONS: NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the REGIONS line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
