BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one mult_add_int_18x19 per lane gn = 0..15 of module norm: clk = clk; reset = (reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress)); mode_sigs = 12'b0; ax = {10'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[gn*8 +: 8])} (the 8-bit difference, truncated before the zero-extension — this operand register is the old mean_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]); ay = {11'b0, (validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1)}; bx = 36'b0; chainin = 64'b0; scanin = 19'b0; result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data[gn*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask (= validity_mask_a_rows) do not change from one cycle to the next while the norm unit is active, i.e. while (enable_norm=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || norm_in_progress)). Support: 5007-5010 and 5894-5906 — both come straight from the cfg block's registers (inv_var at 4764/4823, validity_mask_a_rows at 4769/4829) and are changed only by a well-formed APB write (4874-4883 and the REG_INV_VAR_ADDR / REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR cases of the same case), i.e. they are configuration written before a run; 5059 — the unit only becomes active once the matmul produces matmul_c_data_available (5898), which only follows start_tpu (4884-4885). The original reads mean and the mask at edge k for stage 1 and reads inv_var and the mask again at edge k+1 for stage 2; the block reads all of them at edge k. The two agree exactly whenever the pair is stable across those two edges. mean needs no assumption (it is used only in stage 1, at the same edge k as before).

Converted region — the 16 multiplies at line 5070 (one per lane, 1 instance of norm)

What the original does, per lane i, in the one always block at 5049:

  • branch A (reset || ~enable_norm) and branch C (idle): mean_applied_data <= 0; variance_applied_data <= 0;
  • branch B (active), mask bit 1: M[i] <= inp_data[i] - mean (8-bit, mod 256) and V[i] <= M[i] * inv_var (8-bit, mod 256);
  • branch B, mask bit 0: M[i] <= inp_data[i] and V[i] <= M[i].

mean_applied_data is read nowhere except those stage-2 expressions (5070 and 5074 — the only readers in the file besides its own clears at 5051/5098 and its declaration at 5023), so it may become the block's ax operand register; variance_applied_data is read only at 5107 (assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;), so it may become the block's result. Visibility therefore holds: nothing outside the block reads either value.

Value match: ax carries the 8-bit stage-1 value (the subtraction truncated to 8 bits before the zero-extension, so the block's unsigned 18-bit operand is exactly the bit pattern the original 8-bit register holds), ay carries inv_var for a valid lane and 1 for an invalid one, so result = ax*ay (37 bits, no wrap) and result[7:0] is (M[i]*inv_var) mod 256 for a valid lane and M[i] for an invalid one — the original value in both cases. bx and chainin are tied to 0, so no term is added that the original does not have, and no term of the original is dropped: the mean subtraction stays outside, in the same soft logic the design already has, feeding ax.

Timing/reset match, edge by edge, with the block's reset driven by the clear condition of branches A and C:

  • clear at edge k: block clears ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, chainin_reg and result → result = 0 after k, matching V <= 0; the original's M <= 0 is matched by ax_reg = 0.
  • active at k, cleared at k-1: result after k = ax_reg*ay_reg from k-1 = 0*0 = 0, and the original gives V <= M(=0) * inv_var = 0 (or V <= M(=0) for an invalid lane) = 0. Match.
  • active at k and k+1: result after k+1 = (value loaded into ax at k) * (value loaded into ay at k) = the original's V after k+1 under the ASSUMES above.
    Power-up: the cfg registers come up with enable_norm = 0 and the testbench holds reset high for its first 4 cycles, so the block's reset is high at the first edges and every block register is defined well before the first compared cycle; no X can reach out_data.

Register stages, lane i:

  • Before: inp_data → (stage 1) mean_applied_data → (stage 2) variance_applied_data. Two stages.
  • After: inp_data → (stage 1) the block's ax/ay input registers → (stage 2) the block's result. Two stages, same latency, same values. cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped and inp_data_flopped are untouched, so out_data_available, done_norm and the disabled-block bypass (5031-5033) are unchanged.

Blocks added: 16 (one DSP tile each; 94 tiles are used today and 16 soft multiplies leave the packed pool, so the design stays far inside the 132 tiles). Soft logic removed: the 16 8×8 multiplies and 256 flip-flops (mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data, 128 bits each); added: the 16 8-bit 2:1 muxes that build ay.

Why this region, after the first pass: the measured critical path of pass 1 is reg → $mul~1965.b[0] → $mul~1965.out[7] → reg, with 2.045 ns of wire on the way into the multiply's b operand and the sink at output bit 7. In mean_applied_data * inv_var the b operand is inv_var, which is driven from the cfg block far away and is the only multiply operand in this design that is not a register local to its own module, and the product is truncated to 8 bits, so bit 7 is its top used bit. In a PE, qmult(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped)) (4689, 4723-4728) has both operands in the PE's own registers and uses all 16 product bits. The rewrite puts that long inv_var route in front of the block's input register (0.060 + wire + 0.019 setup ≈ 2.1 ns) and leaves 2.219 ns inside the block, so the path is cut in two. Downstream nothing lengthens: variance_applied_data was a flip-flop output and becomes the block's result register output, the same 0.060 ns clock-to-Q into the same pool/activation logic.

Edits for the editor (module norm, lines 5007-5109 of the unmodified source — change nothing else in the file; in particular do not apply the first pass's activation edit, and do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19, instantiate it by name):

  1. Line 5023: delete the declaration of mean_applied_data. Line 5024: change variance_applied_data from reg to wire [\DESIGN_SIZE*\DWIDTH-1:0]. Line 5048: delete reg [31:0] i; (its only use is the loop deleted below).
  2. In the always block at 5049: delete lines 5051 and 5052 (branch A's two clears), delete the whole for loop at 5067-5076 (its body contains only the two assignments being replaced), and delete lines 5098 and 5099 (branch C's two clears). Keep everything else exactly as it is: out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available and inp_data_flopped <= inp_data (5053-5058, 5060, 5080-5095, 5100-5103).
  3. Between the end of the always block (5105) and assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data; (5107), add:
//The mean-applied stage and the variance multiply are mapped onto one mult_add_int_18x19 per
//lane: the block's ax register holds what mean_applied_data held, result holds variance_applied_data.
wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
genvar gn;
generate
for (gn = 0; gn < `DESIGN_SIZE; gn = gn + 1) begin : norm_dsp
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_nxt =
      validity_mask[gn] ? (inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean)
                        : inp_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH];
  wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] scale_nxt = validity_mask[gn] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
  wire [63:0] blk_result;
  wire [63:0] blk_chainout;
  wire [18:0] blk_scanout;
  mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mul_add (
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(norm_blk_reset),
    .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, mean_applied_nxt}),
    .ay({11'b0, scale_nxt}),
    .bx(36'b0),
    .chainin(64'b0),
    .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(blk_result),
    .chainout(blk_chainout),
    .scanout(blk_scanout));
  assign variance_applied_data[gn*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = blk_result[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

(16 hand-unrolled instances are acceptable if the generate form gives the flow trouble; the connections must be identical. mean_applied_nxt must be declared 8 bits wide so the subtraction truncates to 8 bits before the zero-extension to ax.) chainout/scanout are left dangling — the blocks are not chained, so chainin is tied to 0 as required, and nothing reads chainout.

Candidates left this pass

  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 … $530 (16 multiplies, module activation): left, not chosen this pass. The pass-1 rewrite of them is correct (it passed the reference simulation), but it was measured at +2.08% area and D/D0 = 1.000: the 16 paths it removed were not the critical ones, and each block costs a whole DSP tile where four 8×8 multiplies otherwise share one. Converting them again would only add that area to this pass. It stays available if the norm result is favourable and a second region is worth its tile cost.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349 (256 instances, the systolic array's PEs — 16×16 processing_elements at 4112 ff., each with one seq_mac): left, a capacity reason. The block takes a DSP tile alone and the array needs one per PE, i.e. 256 tiles on a 132-tile device, so the region cannot be converted whole; a subset gains nothing, because the uncut PE multiply paths keep the same period. (Separately, the accumulator at 4697-4704 cannot use bx as its feedback: bx is registered at the block's entry, so the loop would add result from two cycles earlier and split the accumulation into two interleaved sums; the block would have to serve as the multiplier only, which is what makes it one tile per PE.)

DESIGN_CHANGE: (1) The period of this design is set by reg → 8×8 multiply → reg paths, 256 of which are the PE MACs; with one DSP tile per block none of them can move onto this block (256 needed, 132 available). A mode that fits several 8×8 multiply-adds in one tile, or more DSP columns, is what would let the array use it and is the only change that can move this design's critical path. (2) In norm, registering inv_var and validity_mask one stage earlier (a local copy sampled with the same clear/enable as mean_applied_data, used at 5070-5074) would make the block's entry-registered operands exact and remove the need for the ASSUMES above. (3) In activation, assigning the tanh pipeline registers (5482-5485) unconditionally rather than only in the activation_type==1 arm would likewise remove the assumption that pass 1 needed there.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the source before planning.

REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — no block is instantiated by this plan; the kept candidate (revision 1, the 16 norm lanes on 16 blocks) stands as the result.
PLAN:

I propose no further rewrite this pass: the period is now floored by a region that cannot be converted on this device, and every remaining candidate can only add DSP-tile area at an unchanged period. The reasoning, from the source and from the two measurements already taken:

What the current critical path is. The reported path at seed 1 is soft FF (lo17827.Q) → 2.253 ns route → $mul~3943.b[4] → mult (2.140) → 1.349 ns route → soft FF (lo00770.D), with no cell between the multiply output and the flip-flop's D pin. Only one multiply in this design feeds a register with no intervening logic: seq_mac's mul_out_temp_reg <= mul_out_temp; at 4691-4693, fed by qmult mult_u1(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped), ...) at 4689, whose operands are the unconditional registers at 4683-4686. Every other candidate's product lands in a register written inside an if (reset || ~enable_*) ... else if (...) arm (slope_applied_data_internal at 5482 under 5466/5477; variance_applied_data at 5070 under 5050/5059), so a select LUT necessarily sits between the multiply and the flop and would appear as a cell in that path. The limiting path is therefore a PE MAC multiply: a_flopped/b_flopped in a CLB → a mult_9x9_fixed_pt in a DSP column → mul_out_temp_reg in a CLB, 3.6 ns of which is routing between the CLB and the DSP column. It is already the worst path with norm converted, and it was already the worst path in the baseline and in the first pass (which is why the first pass measured D/D0 = 1.000).

Candidate regions, one by one.

  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349 (256 instances — the 16×16 processing_element array at 4112 ff., each with one seq_mac): left, a capacity reason, and it is the only region whose conversion could lower the period. Its register structure is a perfect fit for the block (operands registered at edge k at 4683-4686, product registered at edge k+1 at 4691-4693, i.e. exactly the block's 2-cycle operand-register → result-register contract, with bx = chainin = 0 and result[15:0] replacing mul_out_temp_reg). But the block takes a DSP tile alone and the array needs one per PE: 256 tiles on a 132-tile device. A subset cannot help: the 256 PE multiplies are structurally identical, so every PE left in soft logic keeps a reg → DSP mult → reg path of the same ~5.85 ns, and the period is the worst of them. Converting a subset would raise area (≈ +208k MWTA per PE for the tile, against ~16 flip-flops and a quarter of a shared mult_9x9 tile removed) at an unchanged period. Two PE multiplies also cannot share one block: a propagates horizontally and b vertically one register per hop (4112-4159), so no two PEs hold the same operand value in the same cycle, and there is no shared operand to factor out of an 18×19 product. Likewise the accumulator at 4697-4704 cannot use bx, since bx is registered at the block's entry and the feedback would be two cycles stale.
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 … $530 (16 multiplies, module activation): left, not chosen this pass. This conversion was already built and measured (the first pass): it is correct, but it cost +2.08% area and gave D/D0 = 1.000. Its paths were not critical then, and they are strictly below the PE path now, which is the measured limiter. Adding it to the kept candidate would add 16 DSP tiles' worth of area (≈ +1.8%) with a period that cannot drop below the PE floor, i.e. a predicted product ≈ +1.1% against the kept revision 1 — a pass with a knowable negative outcome, not an experiment.
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368 … $413 (16 multiplies, module norm): already converted — this is the kept candidate (revision 1), 16 blocks, A/A0 1.003, D/D0 0.990, product 0.993. Nothing is left to convert there. It also cannot be made cheaper: two lanes share inv_var, so one might hope to pack lanes p and q as ax = m_p + (m_q << s), but the lane-q field is only clean if s ≥ 16 (each 8×8 product is 16 bits wide and only its low 8 bits are kept), and m_q << 16 does not fit in the 18-bit ax; at any s ≤ 10 the upper bits of m_p*inv_var corrupt the lane-q field and cannot be cancelled, since they are not separately observable at any port. Folding the mean subtraction into the block via bx = -(mean*inv_var) would need a second block's product, which arrives two cycles late at a bx port that is registered at the block's entry. So 16 blocks is the floor for that region and revision 1 is its best form.

Blocks added by this plan: 0. Register stages: unchanged everywhere, since no region is converted this pass; the kept candidate's norm lanes keep their two stages (inp_data → block ax/ay input registers → block result), exactly as the original's mean_applied_data → variance_applied_data.

The run should end and keep revision 1, whose median over all five seeds (3.7642e+08) is below the original's (3.78983e+08). I note plainly that the margin, -0.68%, is inside the ~3.5% seed-to-seed spread quoted for this design, so it is a small and not strongly separated win; it is nonetheless the best measured result and the measurement rule is the median over every seed, which revision 1 satisfies.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The period of this design is set by the 256 PE MAC multiplies, each a CLB-register → DSP-column multiply → CLB-register path with ~3.6 ns of that in routing. Three changes would unlock it, none of which a rewrite may make: (1) a DSP mode that fits several 8×8 multiply-adds in one tile (or ≥256 DSP tiles), so every PE multiply could use the hard block's internal registers and the long CLB↔DSP routes would be cut by the block's own input register; (2) in seq_mac, letting the PE's operand and product registers live with the multiplier (they already form the exact 2-stage pattern the block implements), which is what makes the per-tile cost, not the function, the obstacle; (3) in norm, registering inv_var and validity_mask one stage earlier with the same clear condition as mean_applied_data would remove the ASSUMES the kept candidate needs, and in activation, assigning the tanh pipeline registers (5482-5485) unconditionally would do the same for that region.

Cost a client-side list-price estimate per call, summed, not a bill. Token components are kept separate (uncached input, output, reasoning, cache read, cache write); total is their sum, with reasoning counted once. Reasoning tokens are already counted inside the output column.

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)

Pass 2
loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 6.2834e+07 · critical path 6.03149 ns · wirelength 395418 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 3.78983e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 3.45%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 3.45%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

`activation_type` does not change while the activation unit is processing data, i.e. while (enable_activation=1 && reset=0 && (in_data_available || activation_in_progress)). Support: lines 5480-5489 — when activation_type==0 the tanh pipeline registers (slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed, intercept_applied_data_internal) are not assigned in that branch and therefore *hold*; line 5534 — intercept_applied_data_internal is read only when activation_type==1; line 5461 — out_data exposes it only when enable_activation==1. The rewrite reproduces the "clear" and "load" branches exactly; only the frozen-pipeline (ReLU-mode) branch is not reproduced, and it is observable only if activation_type goes 0→1 without the unit leaving the active state in between. Lines 4838/4913 (activation_type resets to 1 and is only changed by an APB write to `REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR`) and 5259-5262 (the chain only starts on start_tpu) show the CSR is a configuration register written before a run. The plan, in full 6462 chars · not approved

I'll read the source before planning.

REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — no block is instantiated by this plan; the kept candidate (revision 1, the 16 norm lanes on 16 blocks) stands as the result.
PLAN:

I propose no further rewrite this pass: the period is now floored by a region that cannot be converted on this device, and every remaining candidate can only add DSP-tile area at an unchanged period. The reasoning, from the source and from the two measurements already taken:

What the current critical path is. The reported path at seed 1 is soft FF (lo17827.Q) → 2.253 ns route → $mul~3943.b[4] → mult (2.140) → 1.349 ns route → soft FF (lo00770.D), with no cell between the multiply output and the flip-flop's D pin. Only one multiply in this design feeds a register with no intervening logic: seq_mac's mul_out_temp_reg <= mul_out_temp; at 4691-4693, fed by qmult mult_u1(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped), ...) at 4689, whose operands are the unconditional registers at 4683-4686. Every other candidate's product lands in a register written inside an if (reset || ~enable_*) ... else if (...) arm (slope_applied_data_internal at 5482 under 5466/5477; variance_applied_data at 5070 under 5050/5059), so a select LUT necessarily sits between the multiply and the flop and would appear as a cell in that path. The limiting path is therefore a PE MAC multiply: a_flopped/b_flopped in a CLB → a mult_9x9_fixed_pt in a DSP column → mul_out_temp_reg in a CLB, 3.6 ns of which is routing between the CLB and the DSP column. It is already the worst path with norm converted, and it was already the worst path in the baseline and in the first pass (which is why the first pass measured D/D0 = 1.000).

Candidate regions, one by one.

  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349 (256 instances — the 16×16 processing_element array at 4112 ff., each with one seq_mac): left, a capacity reason, and it is the only region whose conversion could lower the period. Its register structure is a perfect fit for the block (operands registered at edge k at 4683-4686, product registered at edge k+1 at 4691-4693, i.e. exactly the block's 2-cycle operand-register → result-register contract, with bx = chainin = 0 and result[15:0] replacing mul_out_temp_reg). But the block takes a DSP tile alone and the array needs one per PE: 256 tiles on a 132-tile device. A subset cannot help: the 256 PE multiplies are structurally identical, so every PE left in soft logic keeps a reg → DSP mult → reg path of the same ~5.85 ns, and the period is the worst of them. Converting a subset would raise area (≈ +208k MWTA per PE for the tile, against ~16 flip-flops and a quarter of a shared mult_9x9 tile removed) at an unchanged period. Two PE multiplies also cannot share one block: a propagates horizontally and b vertically one register per hop (4112-4159), so no two PEs hold the same operand value in the same cycle, and there is no shared operand to factor out of an 18×19 product. Likewise the accumulator at 4697-4704 cannot use bx, since bx is registered at the block's entry and the feedback would be two cycles stale.
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 … $530 (16 multiplies, module activation): left, not chosen this pass. This conversion was already built and measured (the first pass): it is correct, but it cost +2.08% area and gave D/D0 = 1.000. Its paths were not critical then, and they are strictly below the PE path now, which is the measured limiter. Adding it to the kept candidate would add 16 DSP tiles' worth of area (≈ +1.8%) with a period that cannot drop below the PE floor, i.e. a predicted product ≈ +1.1% against the kept revision 1 — a pass with a knowable negative outcome, not an experiment.
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368 … $413 (16 multiplies, module norm): already converted — this is the kept candidate (revision 1), 16 blocks, A/A0 1.003, D/D0 0.990, product 0.993. Nothing is left to convert there. It also cannot be made cheaper: two lanes share inv_var, so one might hope to pack lanes p and q as ax = m_p + (m_q << s), but the lane-q field is only clean if s ≥ 16 (each 8×8 product is 16 bits wide and only its low 8 bits are kept), and m_q << 16 does not fit in the 18-bit ax; at any s ≤ 10 the upper bits of m_p*inv_var corrupt the lane-q field and cannot be cancelled, since they are not separately observable at any port. Folding the mean subtraction into the block via bx = -(mean*inv_var) would need a second block's product, which arrives two cycles late at a bx port that is registered at the block's entry. So 16 blocks is the floor for that region and revision 1 is its best form.

Blocks added by this plan: 0. Register stages: unchanged everywhere, since no region is converted this pass; the kept candidate's norm lanes keep their two stages (inp_data → block ax/ay input registers → block result), exactly as the original's mean_applied_data → variance_applied_data.

The run should end and keep revision 1, whose median over all five seeds (3.7642e+08) is below the original's (3.78983e+08). I note plainly that the margin, -0.68%, is inside the ~3.5% seed-to-seed spread quoted for this design, so it is a small and not strongly separated win; it is nonetheless the best measured result and the measurement rule is the median over every seed, which revision 1 satisfies.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The period of this design is set by the 256 PE MAC multiplies, each a CLB-register → DSP-column multiply → CLB-register path with ~3.6 ns of that in routing. Three changes would unlock it, none of which a rewrite may make: (1) a DSP mode that fits several 8×8 multiply-adds in one tile (or ≥256 DSP tiles), so every PE multiply could use the hard block's internal registers and the long CLB↔DSP routes would be cut by the block's own input register; (2) in seq_mac, letting the PE's operand and product registers live with the multiplier (they already form the exact 2-stage pattern the block implements), which is what makes the per-tile cost, not the function, the obstacle; (3) in norm, registering inv_var and validity_mask one stage earlier with the same clear condition as mean_applied_data would remove the ASSUMES the kept candidate needs, and in activation, assigning the tanh pipeline registers (5482-5485) unconditionally would do the same for that region.
